## Contradictions

- Maven Central search index lists com.cybersource:cybersource-rest-client-java latest version 0.0.76 with timestamp 3 January 2025 · versus · GitHub latest release for CyberSource/cybersource-rest-client-java is 0.0.94 published 2026-09-07 · https://search.maven.org/solrsearch/select?q=g:com.cybersource&rows=20&wt=json · https://api.github.com/repos/CyberSource/cybersource-rest-client-java/releases/latest
- NuGet registration for CyberSource.Rest.Client shows latest version 0.0.1.30 published 6 April 2023 · versus · GitHub latest release for CyberSource/cybersource-rest-client-dotnet is 1.0.2 published 2026-09-10 · https://api.nuget.org/v3/registration5-semver1/cybersource.rest.client/index.json · https://api.github.com/repos/CyberSource/cybersource-rest-client-dotnet/releases/latest
- PyPI shows cybersource-rest-client-python 0.0.79 uploaded 2026-09-15 · versus · GitHub release 0.0.79 for the same SDK is dated 2026-09-07 · https://pypi.org/pypi/cybersource-rest-client-python/json · https://api.github.com/repos/CyberSource/cybersource-rest-client-python/releases/latest
- Visa Acceptance FAQ: 'There are two forms of authentication available:' HTTP Signature and JWT · versus · Visa Acceptance REST guide: HTTP Signature messaging is '[DEPRECATING]' and all merchants must migrate to JWT by March 2027 · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/support/faqs.html · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/docs/vas/en-us/platform/developer/all/rest/rest-getting-started/restgs-intro.html
- Visa Acceptance SDK page 'Product Editions': Node.js (14.21.3 and above) · versus · Same page, Node getting-started step: 'Pre-requisites : Node.js version 6.17.1+' · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/resources/rest-api-sdks.html · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/resources/rest-api-sdks.html
- Visa Acceptance testing guide: 'We have over 20 processors that you can pick from.' · versus · The processor dropdown on the same page lists 14 processors · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/hello-world/testing-guide.html · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/hello-world/testing-guide.html
- github.com/visa/ai README lists reusable npm packages @visa/token-manager, @visa/mcp-client and @visa/api-client · versus · npm registry returns 'Not found' for all three package names (only @visa/cli, @visa/nova-*, @visa/charts exist under the scope) · https://github.com/visa/ai · https://registry.npmjs.org/@visa%2Fapi-client
- Create-a-Project page: 'Currently, deletion of APIs from a project is not supported.' · versus · Developer quick start: the project's 'Sandbox' page lets you 'Delete APIs' · https://developer.visa.com/pages/working-with-visa-apis/create-project · https://developer.visa.com/pages/working-with-visa-apis/visa-developer-quick-start-guide
- Fintech Fast Track page lists eligibility as 'Have raised at least $3M in funding' · versus · Visa Partner Programs page describes Fast Track eligibility as 'raised at least a Series A or USD$1M+ in funding (regional variances apply)' · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/fintech-program.html · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/partner-programs.html
- 2016 TPA FAQ: 'Only Visa clients can register agents. Agents cannot register themselves.' Registration is via Visa Membership Management on Visa Online. · versus · 2026 Visa Partner TPA page: 'A new option available to agents is to register directly with Visa.' via a self-service tool on Visa Partner; the April 2026 Rules name the 'Program Request Management application' as the Member's registration tool. · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/merchants/tpa-registration-program-faqs.pdf · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/third-party-agent-registration.html · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf
- Visa Rules glossary (April 2026) defines Visa Access as the client extranet 'formerly known as Visa Online' · versus · Visa Partner FAQ and Visa Developer pricing page (both live September 2026) still refer to 'Visa Online' services and 'Visa Online Subscriptions'; the 2024 payfac paper points to 'Visa Online' · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf · https://partner.visa.com/site/support-faq.html · https://developer.visa.com/pages/working-with-visa-apis/visa-developer-pricing
- Licensing page names two client types: Principal Clients and Associate Clients · versus · Visa Rules section 2.2.1.1 and glossary name three: Principal-Type, Associate-Type and Participant-Type Members (Participants cannot license a BIN) · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/licensing-program.html · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf
- Visa FY2025 10-K: 'On June 23, 2025, the court denied a motion to dismiss filed by Visa.' · versus · Payments Dive (2025-06-25): Judge Koeltl 'ruled in an order released Tuesday' (Tuesday = June 24, 2025); Bloomberg's URL is dated 2025-06-24 · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm · https://www.paymentsdive.com/news/visa-loses-bid-to-toss-us-debit-lawsuit-DOJ-antitrust-payments-cards/751607/
- Chargebacks911: 'Beginning April 1, 2025, Visa is introducing a 0.05% card brand fee for all CEDP transactions.' · versus · Priority Commerce (search snippet only; page returned HTTP 401): 'Starting April 11, 2025, Visa will begin charging a 0.05% participation fee on CEDP transactions' · https://chargebacks911.com/visa-rule-changes-april-2025/ · https://prioritycommerce.com/resource-center/visa-cedp-2025-credit-card-rules-changes/
- Visa BioCatch release: close expected 'by the end of Visa's fiscal second quarter of 2027' · versus · Digital Transactions: 'The deal is expected to close by the end of March 2027.' (consistent in substance; different phrasing recorded for the timeline) · https://investor.visa.com/news/news-details/2026/Visa-to-Acquire-BioCatch/default.aspx · https://www.digitaltransactions.net/visa-to-acquire-biocatch-and-other-digital-transactions-news-briefs-from-8-3-26/
- Visa FY2025 10-K (Nov 6, 2025): settlements with opt-out merchants representing 'approximately 82%' of Visa-branded volume of opt-outs. · versus · Visa 10-Q for quarter ended June 30, 2026 (Jul 29, 2026): 'approximately 95%' of the same measure. Likely progression over time rather than a conflict, but the two filings state different figures. · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/R30.htm · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316126000104/R25.htm
- Federal Reserve Reg II table 'All networks' row (curl, verbatim): covered average interchange $0.23, exempt $0.51 in 2024. · versus · WebFetch summary of the same page reported network-type rows: dual-message covered $0.22, single-message covered $0.24, exempt dual-message $0.61, exempt single-message $0.26. Not a contradiction in substance (weighted average vs. by network type) but the by-type rows were not captured verbatim. · https://www.federalreserve.gov/paymentsystems/regii-average-interchange-fee.htm · https://www.federalreserve.gov/paymentsystems/regii-average-interchange-fee.htm
- Visa USA interchange schedule is dated 18 April 2026 with rates effective April 18, 2026. · versus · Interlink interchange schedule on the same Visa merchant page still shows 'Rates Effective July 1, 2023' (metadata August 2023). · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/merchants/visa-usa-interchange-reimbursement-fees.pdf · https://usa.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/download/merchants/interlink-interchange-reimbursement-fees.pdf
- Merchant locations: '150+ million merchant locations' (Visa Investor Day, 2025-02-20) and 'more than 150 million merchant partners' (Jaywan release, 2025-03-10) · versus · 'more than 175 million merchant locations worldwide' (10-K FY2025, data as of June 30, 2025); fact sheet footnote says the 175M 'includes an estimated 64 million locations through payment facilitators', so the change is partly methodological · https://s1.q4cdn.com/050606653/files/doc_downloads/2025/02/20/Visa-2025-Investor-Day-Consolidated.pdf · https://www.visa.com/en-ae/newsroom/visa-al-etihad-payments-jaywan-partnership · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/documents/about-visa-factsheet.pdf
- Credentials: '4.7 billion credentials' (Investor Day, Feb 2025) and 'Cards (M) 4,805' for calendar 2024 (10-K comparison table) · versus · 'nearly 5 billion payment credentials' for fiscal 2025 (10-K narrative; fact sheet '~5B' with card data at June 30, 2025) — dated figures, not an error, but three values circulate · https://s1.q4cdn.com/050606653/files/doc_downloads/2025/02/20/Visa-2025-Investor-Day-Consolidated.pdf · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/documents/about-visa-factsheet.pdf
- Total transactions: '300B+ total transactions' (Investor Day, Feb 2025) and 'Total Transactions (B) 311' for calendar 2024 (10-K table) · versus · '329 billion payments and cash transactions' for fiscal 2025 (10-K narrative and fact sheet) — different periods · https://s1.q4cdn.com/050606653/files/doc_downloads/2025/02/20/Visa-2025-Investor-Day-Consolidated.pdf · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/documents/about-visa-factsheet.pdf
- Prosa: 'subject to customary closing conditions, including applicable regulatory approvals' (10-K filed 2025-11-06) and 'expected to close during the second half of 2024' (Visa release 2023-12-15) · versus · Mexico's CNA rejected the acquisition (EPI, 2026-02-23) — status change after the filing; the deal did not close · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm · https://investor.visa.com/news/news-details/2023/Visa-to-Acquire-a-Majority-Interest-in-Prosa-to-Accelerate-Digital-Payments-Adoption-in-Mexico/default.aspx · https://www.electronicpaymentsinternational.com/news/mexico-blocks-visa-prosa-stake/
- Visa Direct endpoints: '11B+ endpoints' and '~10B Visa Direct transactions' for FY24 (Investor Day) · versus · 'approximately 12 billion endpoints' and 'more than 12.5 billion transactions' for FY25 (10-K) — growth between dates · https://s1.q4cdn.com/050606653/files/doc_downloads/2025/02/20/Visa-2025-Investor-Day-Consolidated.pdf · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm
- Visa Rules (18 April 2026 edition) still name 'Discover Financial Services' as a competitive entity whose marks may not appear on Plus cards · versus · Capital One completed its acquisition of Discover Financial Services on May 18, 2025 (context supplied by the study brief; not restated in the Rules) · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf · https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm
- Stripe's VAMP table lists a merchant-level 'Non-compliant' tier at a 0.5% VAMP ratio / count 5 and an 'Excessive' tier at 1.5% (2.2% CEMEA), and says Visa 'may assess fees' above Non-compliant. · versus · Visa's own VAMP fact sheet (May 2025) defines 50bps as the acquirer 'Above Standard' tier only, sets merchant 'Excessive' at 220bps (150bps LAC) falling to 150bps in AP/Canada/EU/US on 1 April 2026, and has no merchant 'Non-compliant' tier. · https://docs.stripe.com/disputes/monitoring-programs · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/visa-perspectives/security-and-trust/documents/visa-acquirer-monitoring-program-fact-sheet-2025.pdf
- Chargebacks911 (David DeCorte, 2026-05-01) states VAMP thresholds 'became effective' April 2026 and enforcement 'officially commenced on October 1, 2025'. · versus · Visa's fact sheet says program thresholds were 'effective 1 June 2025' with an advisory period ending 30 September 2025 and only the merchant Excessive threshold changing on 1 April 2026; Digital Transactions (Tim Tynan, 2025-11-01) says the programme launched 1 April 2025. · https://chargebacks911.com/visa-acquirer-monitoring-program/ · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/visa-perspectives/security-and-trust/documents/visa-acquirer-monitoring-program-fact-sheet-2025.pdf · https://www.digitaltransactions.net/magazine_articles/navigating-the-visa-acquirer-monitoring-program/
- Visa's merchant Dispute Management Guidelines (June 2024) still describe 'Visa Dispute Monitoring Programs' and a Visa Fraud Monitoring Program as separate merchant monitoring. · versus · Visa's VAMP fact sheet (May 2025) says the Visa Fraud Monitoring Program and Visa Dispute Monitoring Program were consolidated into VAMP, and the April 2026 rulebook contains zero references to a Visa Dispute Monitoring Program. · https://usa.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/global/support-legal/documents/merchants-dispute-management-guidelines.pdf · https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/visa-perspectives/security-and-trust/documents/visa-acquirer-monitoring-program-fact-sheet-2025.pdf
- The assignment brief and Visa's security-compliance ecosystem still refer to the gated client portal as 'Visa Online'. · versus · The April 2026 rulebook glossary defines 'Visa Access' as the password-protected client site 'formerly known as Visa Online' (entry last updated Apr 2025). · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf · https://corporate.visa.com/en/resources/security-compliance.html

- developer.visa.com lists Visa Direct as 'available for use by' Merchants and Independent Developers · versus · Visa Direct for Card docs: production requires the Originator to be a licensed Visa Acquirer or sponsored by one · https://developer.visa.com/capabilities/visa_direct · https://developer.visa.com/capabilities/visa_direct/docs
- Visa Partner Programs page: Fintech Fast Track is for fintechs that 'raised at least a Series A or USD$1M+ in funding (regional variances apply)' · versus · Fintech Fast Track programme page: eligibility 'Have raised at least $3M in funding' · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/partner-programs.html · https://partner.visa.com/site/programs/fintech-program.html
- Visa Partner About page still advertises 'Developer Community ... Get support, share ideas and more in our developer-focused community. - The Visa Rules glossary (Apr 2026) defines 'Visa Access' as the client website 'formerly known as Visa Online', and the login page at visaonline.com brands itself 'WELCOME TO VISA ACCESS'. · versus · Visa's community-issuer resources page still says 'Login to Visa Online', 'Access the program through Visa Online (VOL)' and 'Enroll in Visa Online'; the October 2024 Ecosystem Risk Programs Guide defines 'VOL Visa Online (a regional Visa tool...)'; the developer pricing page refers to subscriptions 'through Visa Online'. · https://usa.visa.com/dam/VCOM/download/about-visa/visa-rules-public.pdf · https://usa.visa.com/partner-with-us/community-issuers/resources.html

- developer.visaacceptance.com presents the platform as 'Visa acceptance platform' with footer '© 2021 Visa Acceptance' and links its community to community.developer.cybersource.com. · versus · developer.cybersource.com is still live with identical navigation branded 'Cybersource' ('the secure and trusted technology of Cybersource'), plus a Demo hub and AI Assistant not shown on the Visa Acceptance version; the support KB maintains parallel Cybersource and Visa Acceptance guides, references and status pages. · https://developer.visaacceptance.com/ · https://developer.cybersource.com/
